I used to really look forward to doing those things after being on my feet all day at work. But now... no, I don't really enjoy them. Like I said, I just don't feel anything good or happy when I do them anymore. It's just blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. It's not like I'm crying or feeling down all the time. It's really just that blank feeling I was talking about. I'm not sad, but I'm not happy either. I'm just kind of... empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel interested in doing things?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I'm not really sure. I guess I haven't really noticed if I'm interested or not. I still get up and go to work, do my shifts stocking, and I come home and do whatever needs doing around the house. I just kind of go through the motions. So I don't know if my interest is gone, exactly... I just don't feel much about it either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad?\n\nPatient: I think I just answered that... but no, not really. Like I said, it's not sadness. I'm not crying or feeling down. It's just that empty, blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about three weeks now. It all started right after... well, there was an accident at work. A pallet collapsed and really injured my coworker. I've just felt this way ever since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really. Honestly, that's what made me come in to see you. I feel like the warm part of me just... shut off. Like, I sit with my husband or try to watch my regular shows after work, and I know I should feel comfort, or even just a little happy to be relaxing. But there's just nothing there. I don't enjoy it, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change. My clothes still fit the same, so I think my weight has stayed pretty much what it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really noticed feeling nervous or on edge, if that's what you mean. It's mostly just been that blank feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really done anything to feel guilty about. So, no, guilt isn't really something I'm dealing with right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities, no matter how small they might seem, that you still look forward to or find some pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. Honestly, that's kind of what made me come in. I feel like the warm part of me just... shut off. I'll sit with my husband or try to watch my shows after work, and I know I should feel comfort or even just a little happy to be relaxing, but there's just nothing there right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life, like eating or taking a warm bath, that you still enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not even those. I mean, I eat because I have to, especially being on my feet all day at work, but I don't really care what I'm eating. It doesn't bring me any joy or taste specially good. And a hot shower is just... getting clean so I can go to bed. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's all just flat. I don't really feel anything good from any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm moving slower. I mean, my job at the warehouse keeps me pretty active, so I'm still moving at my normal pace, getting things stocked and all. I just don't feel anything while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most nights, I get home from my late shift and I'm just completely exhausted. I'll lie down, but every time I start to drift off, I just... jerk awake. It's like I think I hear pallets shifting or something falling at work. And even if I do manage to fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Not really most of the time, no. I know I just said I'm exhausted when I get off my shift, but during the actual day... I'm okay. Once I get to the warehouse and start working, I just run on autopilot. I don't feel like I'm dragging while I'm on the clock or anything. It's really just at night and when I first wake up that I feel it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, my thoughts don't feel slow. If anything, my mind is just kind of... blank a lot of the time, like I said, just on autopilot. But when I need to think about where something goes or how many boxes I need, it comes to me just as fast as it always did. So, no, not slower.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or restless more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I'm always moving around at work anyway, stocking shelves and filling orders, so I'm on my feet a lot. But when I'm actually sitting down or off the clock... no, I'm not really fidgety. I don't get that restless feeling like I need to pace or can't sit still. I usually just stay pretty still.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel worthless. I just feel... empty, I guess. But not worthless. I still know I'm doing my job and taking care of what I need to. It's just that none of it really feels like it matters right now. But no, I wouldn't use the word worthless.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
